L1345: The PKPD model was developed based on all clinical studies in participants with hemophilia A and B. The total number of participants and samples used for PKPD analysis are listed in Table 94. The final population PK model was applied to calculate free TFPI for every individual (i.e., empirical Bayesian estimates). Model generated free TFPI was linked to peak thrombin using an E_{max} type of model with free TFPI as the independent variable and observed peak thrombin as the dependent variable. Figure 38 shows the relationship between observed peak thrombin and
L1346: the model calculated free TFPI for hemophilia participant population, which indicates an inhibitory relationship between free TFPI and peak thrombin.
L1347: **Figure 38. Model-Predicted Free TFPI and Observed Peak Thrombin**
L1348: Source: Population Modeling Analysis Report (PMAR Amendment) – Figure 24
L1349: The observed peak thrombin data is faceted by different studies in hemophilia patients. The blue line is population estimates obtained from the PKPD model and shows the overall relationship between model predicted free TFPI and observed peak thrombin values.
L1350: Abbreviations: PKPD, pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics; TFPI, tissue factor pathway inhibitor
L1351: **Figure 39** shows a comparison of the observed peak thrombin values with population predictions (left panel) and individual predictions (right panel) for the PKPD model, which displays a good agreement between observed values and the population/individual predictions from the model for peak thrombin values.
L1352: **Figure 39. Goodness-of-Fit Plots With Population and Individual Predictions for Peak Thrombin Concentrations**
L1353: Source: Population Modeling Analysis Report (PMAR Amendment) – Figure 21
Line of unity is shown in gray. Loess fit of data is shown in blue.
L1354: The review team found the Applicant's PKPD model for free TFPI and thrombin peak acceptable with an adequate fit of the data as evident. In addition to the PKPD analyses, the review team explored conventional E-R analysis using PK exposure metrics (i.e., C_{\text{avg,ss}}) and the primary efficacy endpoint of ABR to better understand the appropriateness of the proposed dosing regimen of marstacimab.
L1355: ## 14.5.2. Exposure-Response PK Analysis
L1356: The Applicant conducted the E-R analysis of efficacy using marstacimab PK and the primary efficacy endpoint, annualized bleed rate (ABR). The objective of the Applicant's E-R analysis was to support the dose justification for adult and adolescent participants with hemophilia.
L1357: ## Method
L1358: A Poisson regression analysis was used to describe the relationship between marstacimab exposure metrics (steady state C_{\text{avg}}, C_{\text{max}} and C_{\text{min}}) and number of bleeds for hemophilia participants. Doses evaluated were 150 mg SC QW (loading dose of 300 mg SC), 300 mg SC QW and 450 mg SC QW. The recommended clinical dose is 150 mg SC QW (loading dose of 300 mg SC), with a provision to escalate the dose to 300 mg SC QW. The marstacimab C_{\text{avg,ss}} was calculated using post-hoc estimates obtained from the final PPK model. Age was explored in the model as a continuous covariate (not as separate age groups) and showed only a small effect on ABR.
L1359: ## Results
L1360: The E-R analysis included number of bleeds for hemophilia patients in Phase 2 (Studies B7841002 and B7841003) and Phase 3 (Study B7841005) studies. The final model was a Quasi-Poisson regression model with log PK as the independent variable and ABR as the dependent
L1361: ## HYMPAVZI (marstacimab-hncq) injection
L1362: variable, which did not include any covariates. Figure 40 shows the model fits of the final Quasi-Poisson model for marstacimab C_{\text{avg,ss}} vs. ABR, which indicates a negative relationship between examined marstacimab exposure and ABR.
L1363: **Figure 40. Model Fits for the Final E-R Model of ABR With Log of C_{\text{avg}} at Steady-State**
L1364: Source: Response to FDA Information Request-Received February 29, 2024 – Question 1, Figure 1
The x-axis shows steady state exposure metric in ng/mL of C_{\text{avg,ss}}. The y-axis shows ABR values (predicted from the Quasi-Poisson model or observed in the analysis population). The solid circles and error-bars (in black) show the observed mean ABR and 95% CI, respectively for each bin of exposure metric in the analysis population. The blue line, (and blue shaded area) are the model-predicted mean (and 95% CI) ABR for the range of observed exposure metric in the analysis population. The numbers above the error bars show the observed mean ABR values for each bin of exposure metric.
L1365: Abbreviations: ABR, annualized bleeding rates, C_{\text{avg}}, average plasma concentration; C_{\text{avg,ss}}, average plasma concentration at steady-state, CI, confidence interval; E-R, exposure-response
L1366: To evaluate whether the proposed dose is reasonable, E-R analysis was conducted with primary efficacy endpoint of ABR. The LOESS nonparametric regression method with data from the Phase 1b/2 study (Study B7841002), Phase 2 open-label extension study (Study B7841003), and Phase 3 study (Study B7841005) for doses 150, 300, and 450 mg was used in the E-R analysis. Fourteen participants who had dose escalated during the Phase 3 study were not included due to limited PK samples after dose escalation. Figure 41 shows that there is a slight trend for decrease in ABR with increase in marstacimab exposure (C_{\text{avg,ss}}).
L1367: **Figure 41. E-R Analysis for Primary Efficacy Endpoint of Annualized Bleeding Rate (ABR) With Three Marstacimab Administrations**
L1368: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L1369: Loess fit is shown by the blue line in the top plot.
L1370: Abbreviations: C_{\text{avg}}, average plasma concentration; E-R, exposure-response; LD, loading dose
L1371: The review team found a similar trend as the Applicant's model, results of the E-R analysis show a trend for decreasing ABR with increasing concentrations, which is also consistent with the results from the previous PKPD analysis.
L1372: ## 14.5.2.1. Phase 3 Dose Justification for Adolescents
L1373: The justification for same dose (150 mg SC once weekly, with 300 mg SC loading dose) for adolescents and adults in the Phase 3 study (Study B7841005) was based on the predictions from the previous PPK and PKPD model. To understand the weight effect on PK exposure metrics (steady state C_{\text{avg}}, C_{\text{max}} and C_{\text{min}}), the Applicant simulated a population with a uniform weight distribution in the range of 30 to 120 kg. 5000 participants were simulated in total, with approximately 500 participants in each weight category (every 10 kg increment). The pharmacokinetic exposure metrics were predicted from the final PPK model.
L1374: Box plots of simulated steady-state average for 150 mg SC QW across weight categories with observed data overlaid are shown in Figure 42. PK simulations performed for weight categories based on observed weight distribution show that exposures (median C_{\text{avg,ss}}) were predicted to be around 4-fold higher for the 30 to 50 kg group and about 6-fold lower for the 100 to 130 kg group in comparison to predicted exposures for a reference (standard) weight group of 70 to 80 kg. In general, at lower weights, the higher concentrations were still below the maximum concentrations seen in clinical studies. At higher weights, concentrations were above the marstacimab plasma EC_{50} based on modeling of Phase 1 and 2 clinical data. In summary, an overlap was seen for the observed range of concentrations across the 40 to 50 kg to 80 to 90 kg weight categories.
L1375: **Figure 42. Box Plots of Marstacimab C_{\text{avg,ss}} (150 mg SC QW) for Weight Categories (Every 10 kg) in the Range of 30 to 130 kg**
L1376: Source: Response to FDA Information Request-Received February 29, 2024 – Question 2, Figure 1
The plot shows simulated steady state marstacimab average concentration (C_{\text{avg,ss}}) grouped into different weight buckets of 10 kg with weights ranging from 30 kg to 130 kg. Closed circles show the outliers from simulated average marstacimab concentrations. Open circles show observed steady state average marstacimab concentrations. EC_{50} (marstacimab concentration in \mu\text{g/mL}) is based on modeling of animal and Phase 1/Phase 2 clinical data. The numbers above or below the bars show the number of participants in each weight category.
L1377: Abbreviations: C_{\text{avg,ss}}, average plasma concentration at steady state; EC_{50}, dose required for 50% of the population to obtain the therapeutic effect; n, number of participants in the subgroup; QW, once weekly; SC, subcutaneous
L1378: The review team found the Applicant's exposure response model to be acceptable. A slight trend for decreasing ABR with increasing concentrations was found by the E-R analyses. The 150 mg SC QW weekly with 300 mg SC loading dose is acceptable for most patients with a dose escalation that may benefit a small proportion of patients whose ABR is not adequately controlled. Overall, E-R and PKPD analyses support the proposed dosing regimen of marstacimab for the proposed indication, which is based upon the favorable safety and efficacy results of the studied clinical studies.
L1379: ## 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1380: Not applicable.
L1381: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L1382: ## 15.1. Clinical Protocol Synopsis, Study B7841005, Based on Amendment 7, March 24, 2022)
L1383: **Table 98. Study Details, Study B7841005**
L1384: Method	Details
Name of sponsor/company	Pfizer Inc.
Study number	B7841005
Study title	An Open-Label Study in Adolescent and Adult Severe (Coagulation Factor Activity <1%) Hemophilia A Participants With or Without Inhibitors or Moderately Severe to Severe Hemophilia B Participants (Coagulation Factor Activity \le2%) With or Without Inhibitors Comparing Standard Treatment to PF-06741086 Prophylaxis
Name of study intervention	PF-06741086 (marstacimab)
Investigational sites	This is an international, multicenter study to be conducted at approximately 52 Sites.
Phase	3
L1385: Source: FDA table based on Clinical Protocol
L1386: ## Rationale
L1387: Treatment with marstacimab is anticipated to demonstrate a clinically relevant advantage or a major contribution to patient care in comparison to current methods of treatment for hemophilia A or B because it works differently than factor replacement products and its anticipated efficacy is not expected to be impacted by the presence of inhibitors. The potential for once weekly (QW) subcutaneous (SC) administration provides for treatment options in the absence of reliable vascular access, increased convenience and may enable better compliance. Combined, these qualities should result in a reduction of bleeding episodes.
L1388: ## Objectives
L1389: ## **Primary Objectives**
L1390: - To demonstrate the efficacy and safety of marstacimab for routine prophylaxis in severe hemophilia A or moderately severe to severe hemophilia B (FVIII activity &lt;1% or FIX activity \le2%, respectively) participants 12 to &lt;75 years of age with or without inhibitors
L1391: ## **Secondary Objectives**
L1392: - To evaluate additional efficacy of marstacimab
- To evaluate the effect of marstacimab on Health-related quality of life (HRQoL)
L1393: ## **Tertiary/Exploratory Objectives**
L1394: - Assess PK and PD parameters in this study population
- To evaluate the effect of marstacimab on Health-related quality of life
- To compare joint health post marstacimab to baseline
- To evaluate the effect of marstacimab on total coagulation factor or bypass product consumption
L1395: ## **Overall Design**
L1396: This is a one-way, cross-over, open-label, multi-center study in approximately 145 adolescent and adult participants between ages 12 to &lt;75 years with severe hemophilia A or moderately severe to severe hemophilia B (defined as FVIII activity &lt;1% or FIX activity ≤2%, respectively) with or without inhibitor, with approximately 20% of participants as adolescents (ages between 12 to &lt;18 years old). Additional participants (approximately 20%) may be recruited for this study in order to account for attrition of enrolled participants and to provide for sufficient representation into regions which have experienced recruitment delays due to the COVID-19 pandemic. This study is comparing treatment with their prescribed factor replacement therapy or bypass therapy during an observational phase with a 12-month Active Treatment Phase, during which participants will receive prophylaxis (defined as treatment by SC injection of marstacimab [PF-06741086]).
L1397: The dosing regimen is marstacimab 300 mg SC for initial loading dose followed by 150 mg SC QW. Individual participants who meet protocol specified dose escalation criteria based upon breakthrough bleeding, may have the dose increased to 300 mg SC QW.
L1398: ## **Disclosure Statement**
L1399: This is a Cross-Over Prevention study with one arm which has no masking
L1400: ## **Number of Participants**
L1401: - Approximately 145 participants (approximately 20% adolescents) will be enrolled:
- Approximately 45 participants (approximately 35 hemophilia A and 10 hemophilia B participants) in the Inhibitor Cohort;
- Approximately 100 participants (approximately 80 hemophilia A and 20 hemophilia B participants) in the Non-Inhibitor Cohort.
- The Inhibitor Cohort (n=45) will include at least 5 adolescent participants and the Non-Inhibitor Cohort will include at least 20 adolescent participants.
L1402: ## **Intervention Groups and Duration**
L1403: Enrollment of individuals into the Observational Phase who are on prior on-demand or prophylaxis treatment will begin at the same time.
L1404: ## **Prior On-Demand Treatment**
L1405: Participants with either hemophilia A or B, with or without inhibitors, and who are prescribed an on-demand treatment regimen during the observational phase will transition to the Active Treatment Phase after approximately 6 months. Individuals with inhibitors will receive on-demand treatment with recombinant factor VIIa (rFVIIa), Prothrombin Complex Concentrates (PCC), or activated Prothrombin Complex Concentrates (aPCC) agents e.g., FEIBA or in regions where available, BYCLOT (Inhibitor Cohort) and individuals without inhibitors will receive on on-demand treatment with FVIII- or FIX-replacement therapy (noninhibitor cohort) during the observational phase before crossing over to the 12-month active treatment phase.
L1406: ## **Prior Prophylaxis Treatment**
L1407: Participants without inhibitors who were on prior prophylactic treatment with FVIII- or FIX-replacement (defined as treatment by intravenous injection of factor concentrate to prevent bleeding) during the observational phase will transition to the Active Treatment Phase after approximately 6 months.
L1408: ## **Study Duration**
L1409: The anticipated study duration for an individual participant is approximately 21 months, including an approximately 45-day Screening period, an observational phase of approximately 6 months, a 12-month active treatment phase during which the participant receives an initial loading dose followed by prophylaxis treatment with PF-06741086, and a 1-month follow-up for safety monitoring.
L1410: Figure 43. Phase 3 Study Schematic
L1411: ## Phase 3 Study Schematic
L1412: *Remaining 5 participants will be allocated to the non-inhibitor cohorts at the discretion of the sponsor.
Note: Participants with FVIII or FIX inhibitors who are treated with routine prophylaxis may be enrolled, on a case-by-case basis (to supplement the safety analysis of the inhibitor cohorts) after discussion and agreement from the Pfizer medical monitor.
L1413: Source: Study B7841005 Protocol schema
L1414: Abbreviations: FIX, factor IX; FVIII, factor VIII; Hem A, hemophilia A; Hem B, hemophilia B; N, overall number of participants; n, number of participants in subgroup
L1415: ## Study Population
L1416: ## Inclusion Criteria
L1417: - Male age 12 to &lt;75 years inclusive with a minimum body weight of 35 kg.
- Participants with a diagnosis of severe hemophilia A or moderately severe to severe hemophilia B (FVIII activity &lt;1% or FIX activity \le 2\%, respectively) documented by clinical laboratory prior to enrollment. The severity of hemophilia may be confirmed either by documented historical evidence from a clinical laboratory prior to Screening or by factor activity obtained from a clinical laboratory (which may include the central laboratory for this study) prior to enrollment.
- Participants who are enrolled into the non-inhibitor cohort must also meet the following criteria:
L1418: ## HYMPAVZI (marstacimab-hncq) injection
L1419: - No detectable or documented history of inhibitors (\ge 0.6 BU/mL or greater than the upper limit of normal [ULN] for the testing laboratory) against FVIII or FIX prior to enrollment (baseline of observational phase).
- Subjects receiving routine prophylaxis treatment with FVIII/FIX replacement, have demonstrated at least 80% compliance with scheduled prophylaxis regimen during 6 months prior to enrollment, and willing to continue to receive routine prophylaxis treatment with FVIII/FIX replacement during the observational phase.
L1420: OR
L1421: - Subject with on-demand treatment regimen with \ge 6 acute bleeding episodes (spontaneous and/or traumatic) that required coagulation factor infusion during the 6 months period prior to screening and willing to continue to receive on-demand treatment during the observational phase. Surgical bleeding episodes do not apply to this criterion.
L1422: ## **Exclusion Criteria**
L1423: - Previous or current treatment for or history of coronary artery diseases, venous or arterial thrombosis (Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events [CTCAE] Grade &gt;1), or ischemic disease (except for catheter-associated thrombosis).
- Known planned surgical procedure during the planned study period.
- Known hemostatic defect other than hemophilia A or B.
- Abnormal renal or hepatic function as defined by the following laboratory results at screening:Alanine transaminase (ALT) &gt;2 \times ULN.
- Bilirubin &gt;1.5 \times ULN (isolated bilirubin &gt;1.5 \times ULN is acceptable if bilirubin is fractionated and direct bilirubin &lt;35\%).
- Current unstable liver or biliary disease per investigator assessment defined by the presence of ascites, encephalopathy, coagulopathy, hypoalbuminaemia, esophageal or gastric varices, persistent jaundice, or cirrhosis. NOTE: Stable chronic liver disease (including Gilbert's syndrome, asymptomatic gallstones, and chronic stable hepatitis B or C -eg, presence of hepatitis B surface antigen [HBsAg] or positive hepatitis C antibody test result at screening or within 3 months prior to starting study intervention) is acceptable if the participant otherwise meets entry criteria.Serum albumin less than the lower limit of normal (LLN).
- Estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR) &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m^2.
- Abnormal hematology values as defined by the following laboratory tests at Screening:Platelet count &lt;100,000/\mu\text{L}
- Hemoglobin level &lt;10 g/dL
- Other acute or chronic medical or psychiatric condition including recent (within the past year) or active suicidal ideation or behavior or laboratory abnormality that may increase the risk associated with study participation or investigational product administration or may interfere with the interpretation of study results and, in the judgment of the investigator, would make the participant inappropriate for entry into this study.
L1424: ## HYMPAVZI (marstacimab-hncq) injection
L1425: - QTcF &gt;450 msec or QTcF &gt;480 msec in subjects with bundle branch block.
- Participants with hypersensitivity or an allergic reaction to hamster protein or other components of the study intervention.
- Current routine prophylaxis with bypassing agent (e.g., aPCC, BYCLOT, Prothrombin Complex Concentrates [PCC], or rFVIIa), noncoagulation nonfactor replacement therapy (e.g., emicizumab), or any previous treatment with a gene therapy product for treatment of hemophilia.Participants with inhibitors who are being treated using a prophylaxis treatment regimen with a bypass agent will be considered on a case-by-case basis, only after discussion and agreement between the investigator and the Pfizer medical monitor.
- Participants who have previously received nonfactor-based hemophilia therapy (e.g., fitusiran, concizumab, emicizumab) will be considered on a case-by-case basis, only after discussion and agreement between the investigator and the Pfizer medical monitor.
- Regular, concomitant therapy with immunomodulatory drugs (e.g., IV immunoglobulin [IVIG], and routine systemic corticosteroids, rituximab).
- Ongoing or planned use of immune tolerance induction during the OP or ATP, or prophylaxis with FVIII or FIX replacement at any time after initiation of treatment with study intervention during the ATP.
- Participation in other studies involving investigational drug(s) or investigational vaccine(s) within 30 days (or as determined by local requirements) or 5 half-lives prior to study entry or during study participation.
- Previous exposure to marstacimab during to participation in Studies B7841002 and B7841003.
- CD4 cell count \le 200/\mu\text{L} if human immunodeficiency virus (HIV)-positive.
- Screening 12-lead ECG that demonstrates clinically relevant abnormalities that may affect participant safety or interpretation of study results.
L1426: ## **Treatment of Breakthrough Bleeding**
L1427: During OP: Patients experience acute bleeding episode should be stabilized utilizing the patient's standard hemostatic treatment regimen, which may include FVIII- or FIX replacement therapy.
L1428: During the ATP: Patients experience breakthrough bleeding judge by the investigator or treating physician to require additional hemostatic therapy beyond PF-06741086 prophylaxis, FVIII- or FIX- or FIX-replacement products may be administered at the lowest effective dose and according to the approved product labeling.
L1429: ## **Prohibited Medications**
L1430: Immunomodulatory medications (e.g., IVIG, routine systemic corticosteroids, rituximab), emicizumab, bypassing agent (rFVIIa, PCC, aPCC, or BYCLOT) were prohibited throughout all phases of the study.
L1431: Systemic antifibrinolytic agents or medications known to influence platelet function (e.g., aspirin or certain non-steroidal anti-inflammatory drugs) require a washout of \ge 120 hours prior to
L1432: administration of study intervention on Day 0 through completion of ATP Day 360 (Visit 20) in the Active Treatment Phase.
L1433: ## **Study Intervention Compliance**
L1434: Participant compliance with completion of eDiary and study intervention injections will be assessed at each visit. Compliance will be assessed by review of the eDiary. Participants must maintain at least 80% compliance with weekly dosing of PF-06741086.
L1435: ## **Dose Modification**
L1436: The dose may be escalated after 6 months of active treatment with marstacimab, and at any time after completion of Visit 14 (ATP Day 180), from 150 mg SC QW to 300 mg SC QW if the following criterion is met:
L1437: - Weigh at least 50 kg and not adolescent.
- Two or more spontaneous (atraumatic) bleeds (consisting of joint bleeds or significant soft tissue/muscle or other site bleeds) treated with infusion(s) of coagulation FVIII or FIX over a 6-month period in the absence of a confirmed FVIII or FIX inhibitor, respectively.

ECG	X	TriPLICATE ECGs will be obtained, approximately 2-4 minutes apart, at the same time as collection of all cTnI samples when the participant is in a supine position, including scheduled sample collection and any follow-up sample collection performed during study participation. The average of the triplicate ECG measurements collected at each nominal time point during Observational Phase Visit 1 will serve as each participant's baseline value (see Appendix 9 for examples of abnormalities).
At sites where cTnI results are available and when abnormal, triplicate ECGs should be obtained if the participant is available to return for optional repeated cTnI sample collection within 24 hours. Interpretation for all abnormal ECGs and any ECGs obtained at the time of an abnormal cTnI laboratory value will be provided by central reader at the core ECG laboratory.

All other doses will be administered by the participant/caregiver and documented in the eDiary. Participant or caregiver will be retrained on study intervention administration, if required.

L1478: ## 16. Efficacy
L1479: ## 16.1. Derivation of the Noninferiority Margins for Major Efficacy Endpoints for Routine Prophylaxis
L1480: The section described the Applicant's derivation of the noninferiority (NI) margins for the bleeding-related primary and key secondary endpoints for routine prophylaxis.
L1481: ## Noninferiority Margin for the Primary Endpoint (ABR for Treated Bleeds)
L1482: In the statistical analysis plan (SAP) v6 dated January 31, 2023, the Applicant stated that historical data were selected from among the Applicant's hemophilia studies. This enabled selection of individual participant level data that would closely match the inclusion/exclusion criteria of Study B7841005. In selecting studies, the following aspects were considered: (1) having both prophylaxis and on-demand treatment, providing a within-study treatment difference estimate, (2) having an overlapping age group of 12 to 74 years old to match Study B7841005
L1483: age group, and (3) not being a regional study. The following three studies satisfied these criteria: B1821010 (BenefIX), B1821002 (BenefIX), and B1831004 (Xyntha/ReFacto).
L1484: - B1821010 was an open-label 1-way crossover study comparing the efficacy and safety between on-demand treatment and prophylactic therapy using BenefIX in hemophilia B participants age 12 to 65 years old and factor IX activity level not greater than 2%. This study comprised Period 1 (on-demand treatment at a dosage determined by principal investigator, 6 months) and Period 2 (100 international units [IU]/kg once-weekly dose, 12 months).
- B1821002 was an open-label, randomized, 4-period crossover study in males with hemophilia B, age 6 to 65 years old with factor IX activity level not greater than 2% intended to compare on-demand administration and two prophylaxis regimens. The study was comprised the following four periods:Period 1: On-demand administration per dose directed by investigators, 16 weeks.
- Period 2: 50 IU/kg/ twice weekly or 100 IU/kg/once weekly (randomized), 16 weeks.
- Period 3: On-demand administration per dose directed by investigators, 8 weeks.
- Period 4: 50 IU/kg/twice weekly or 100 IU/kg/once weekly (different regimen than that applied during Period 2), 16 weeks.
- B1831004 evaluated the safety and efficacy of ReFacto AF in hemophilia A participants switching to ReFacto AF from ReFacto or other factor VIII products in usual care settings. The participants were age 12 and greater and had factor XIII activity level &lt;1%. All participants were treated under standard care with ReFacto AF at a dose and frequency prescribed by the treating physician. The duration of treatment depended on the frequency of infusions (to achieve 100 exposure days) with mean (SD) of 336 (223) days.
L1485: Table 100 presents the Applicant's analyses of the ABR of treated bleeds based on participant-level data from three hemophilia studies with inclusion/exclusion criteria that closely matched those for Study B7841005. The mean ABR difference between prophylaxis and on-demand treatment was estimated using three analyses: t-test allowing for nonhomogeneous variances, Hodges-Lehman location-shift parameter estimation, and additive negative binomial model. All analyses resulted in similar estimated mean differences of approximately 21, and the lower bound of 2-sided 95% confidence intervals ranging from 16.8 to 17.6.
L1486: During the review, the review team sent an IR and requested the Applicant to (1) clarify if the three studies [i.e., B1821010 (BenefIX), B1821002 (BenefIX), and B1831004 (Xyntha/ReFacto)] were based on hemophilia A or B participants with and without inhibitors, or based only on participants without inhibitors; and (2) provide the potential NI margins for all major efficacy endpoints based only on participants without inhibitors. The Applicant responded that the three internal studies included only enrolled participants without inhibitors.

L1496: Based on the lower bound of the confidence interval of this estimate, the ABR was assumed to be higher in patients receiving on-demand compared to prophylaxis by at least 16.8, which was considered margin 1 (M_1) in the noninferiority test setting.
L1497: Given the large effect size of prophylaxis treatment, an appropriate value for margin 2 (M_2) was considered in order to preserve a sufficiently large proportion of this effect. A value of 2.5 for M_2 (~85% effect preserved) was selected as both clinically meaningful and yielding a reasonable sample size.
L1498: ## **Noninferiority Margin for Key Secondary Endpoint (ABR for Joint Bleeds)**
L1499: The Applicant conducted analyses based on the two BeneFIX studies since the ReFacto study did not utilize this endpoint. All analyses resulted in similar estimated mean ABR differences of approximately 21 with the lower bound of 2-sided 95% confidence intervals ranging from 15.2 to 16.5. Therefore, the on-demand ABR was assumed to be higher than the prophylaxis ABR by at least 15.2, which was considered M_1 in the noninferiority test setting. Given the large effect size of prophylaxis treatment, an appropriate value for M_2 was considered in order to preserve a sufficiently large proportion of this effect. A value of 2.5 for M_2 (~84% effect preserved) was selected as clinically meaningful for a planned sample size of 60.
L1500: ## **Noninferiority Margin for Key Secondary Endpoint (ABR for Spontaneous Bleeds)**
L1501: The Applicant conducted analyses based on the two BeneFIX studies since the ReFacto study did not utilize this endpoint. Results from the t-test and negative binomial regression were similar with the estimated ABR mean difference of 18.6 and similar 95% CIs. The result from Hodges-
L1502: ## HYMPAVZI (marstacimab-hncq) injection
L1503: Lehman estimation was larger with 21.8 (16.0, 27.4) for the estimated ABR median difference and its 95% CI.
L1504: All analyses indicated that the on-demand ABR was higher than the prophylaxis ABR by at least 13.2, which was considered M_1 in the noninferiority test setting. Given the large effect size of prophylaxis treatment, an appropriate value for M_2 was considered in order to preserve a sufficiently large proportion of this effect. A value of 2.5 for M_2 (~81% effect preserved) was selected as clinically meaningful for a planned sample size of 60.
L1505: ## **Noninferiority Margin for Key Secondary Endpoint (ABR for Target Joints Bleeds)**
L1506: The incidence of target joint bleeds was not an efficacy endpoint in any of the three hemophilia studies in which the estimates for several bleed count endpoints were derived. In the absence of internal data, the Applicant relied on the results of emicizumab publications of HAVEN1 and HAVEN3 studies. The Applicant assumed that the ratio of the effect expressed as the difference between the on-demand treatment versus emicizumab in target joint bleeds over that in joint bleeds would be applicable to characterize the marstacimab effect of the same endpoints. The effect in target joint bleeds is consistently 49% of that in joint bleeds. Therefore, the noninferiority margin of 1.2, derived as 49% of the margin of 2.5 for the joint bleeds, was selected for target joint bleeds, expecting that a similar magnitude (~84%) of the effect would be preserved.

L1510: In the Applicant's derivation of NI margin for target joint bleeds endpoint, both inhibitor and noninhibitor populations were used to provide a more stable estimate of the ratio. If only noninhibitor participants were considered (HAVEN 3), there would have no change in NI margin for target joint bleeds.
L1511: ## **Noninferiority Margin for Key Secondary Endpoint (ABR for Total Bleeds)**
L1512: The incidence of target joint bleeds was not an efficacy endpoint in any of the three hemophilia studies in which the estimates for several bleed count endpoints were derived. In the absence of internal data, the Applicant relied on the results of emicizumab publications of HAVEN1 and HAVEN3 studies. The Applicant assumed that the ratio of the effect expressed as the difference between the on-demand treatment versus emicizumab in total bleeds over that in treated bleeds would be applicable to characterize the marstacimab effect of the same endpoints. The effect in total bleeds exceeds 12% to 23% that in treated bleeds, resulting in the geometric mean of a 17% increase. Therefore, the noninferiority margin of 2.9, derived as 117% of the margin of 2.5 for
L1513: the treated bleeds, was selected for total bleeds expecting that a similar magnitude (~85%) of the effect would be preserved.

L1517: In the Applicant's derivation of NI margin for total bleeds endpoint, both inhibitor and noninhibitor populations were used to provide a more stable estimate of the ratio. However, if only the noninhibitor participants were considered (HAVEN3), the updated NI margin would be higher (3.1) as derived as 123% of the margin 2.5.

L1552: ## 16.4. Results for Superiority Over Factor Prophylaxis
L1553: As noted in Section 6.2.1.4 with the discussion of hierarchical testing and multiple testing strategies, the review team considered Study B7841005 noninhibitor cohorts as the equivalent of independent substudies, and individual alpha (0.05) could be tested to each cohort for superiority versus on-demand factor and noninferiority/superiority versus factor prophylaxis separately. The results of the analysis of superiority versus factor prophylaxis did not contribute to the Agency's regulatory decision and are therefore not included in Section 6. The results of the superiority over factor prophylaxis analysis are presented here for completeness.
L1554: Table 105 presents the results for parametric and nonparametric analyses for the primary (ABR treated bleeds) and key secondary ABR endpoints. For the primary endpoint, the Wilcoxon signed rank test had a large p-value of 0.0788 (versus 0.0376 based on the parametric method, that is, the negative binomial regression model). Similarly, for the secondary endpoint ABR of total bleeds, the Wilcoxon signed rank test had a large p-value of 0.0573 (versus 0.0406 based on the negative binomial regression model). The results for the treated bleeds and total bleeds showed nominally statistical significance for superiority over routine prophylaxis but not in the results based on the Wilcoxon signed rank test. In addition, the small nominal p-values for the treated bleeds and total bleeds may be due to extreme high ABRs during the OP in the HA population. The results for other types of bleeds (spontaneous bleeds, joint bleeds, and target joint bleeds) were nominally not statistically significant for superiority over routine prophylaxis regardless of the parametric and nonparametric methods were used.
L1555: The review team acknowledged that the nonparametric test could be less powerful and thus had larger p-values. However, overall, the results were determined not to be robust because parametric and nonparametric methods reached different conclusions. Therefore, the superiority over routine prophylaxis could not be claimed.

L1563: ## 16.5. Sensitivity Analysis for Extreme Values
L1564: Figure 50 shows the distribution of ABRs for participants in cohort OP-PPX by hemophilia type. For HA and HB, the distribution is shown for OP (lower graph) and ATP (upper graph). As seen in the figure, the distributions of the primary endpoint (ABR for treated bleeds) for HA and HB populations are different. The range of ABRs is wider in HA participants compared to HB participants. This would be expected as the HA phenotype is generally more severe than HB phenotype, and so higher ABRs would be expected both in the OP and the ATP. However, in Study B7841005, for the HA population, the distribution tail, with several extreme values, is longer for the OP compared to the ATP. In contrast in the HB population, the tail, with several extreme values, is longer in ATP compared to OP.
L1565: When there are extreme values in the ATP not seen in the OP, as with the HB population, the mean difference of the ABR is pushed higher, suggesting poorer bleeding control with marstacimab prophylaxis compared to factor prophylaxis. This is discussed as a key efficacy issue in Section 6.3.1. When there are extreme values in the OP not seen the ATP, as with the HA population, the mean difference of the ABR is pushed lower, suggesting poorer bleed control with factor prophylaxis compared to marstacimab prophylaxis. This can be due to poor compliance with the OP or chance.
L1566: To assess the robustness of the final results and assay sensitivity for extreme values (ABR&gt; 40 for HA, ABR &gt; 10 for HB), the Applicant, at the request of the Agency, conducted additional sensitivity analyses for the primary endpoint using the Negative Binomial Regression Model and Wilcoxon Signed Rank test, using different imputation methods (mean, median, and zero ABR imputation) for extreme ABRs. Specifically, imputations were requested using different cutoffs to determine extreme ABRs considering HA and HB separately and HA and HB together. The Applicant conducted the requested analyses by imputing the mean (or median) from the remaining (non-imputed) participants based on the pooled HA and HB populations.
L1567: According to the Applicant's analysis results, non-inferiority vs factor prophylaxis was met for all imputation methods for all scenarios. However, the review team determined this approach, which pools HA and HB ABRs in calculating the imputed value, may not be appropriate given the differences in the ranges of the ABR distributions between HA and HB. Thus, the review team conducted additional, more conservative, sensitivity analyses by replacing the extreme ABRs by mean (or median) of the ABRs of remaining participants with the corresponding hemophilia A (or B) type. That is, extreme HA ABRs are replaced by the mean (median) ABR of the remaining HA participants and HB ABRs are replaced by the mean (median) ABR of the remaining HB participants. Non-inferiority versus factor prophylaxis was also met for all scenarios using these imputation methods.
L1568: In conclusion, these additional sensitivity analyses using parametric and non-parametric analyses with different imputation methods for extreme ABRs in HA and HB populations supported the final results for noninferiority versus factor prophylaxis for the primary endpoint (ABR for treated bleeds).
L1569: Figure 50. ABR Treated Bleed Frequency Distribution by Phase, Hemophilia A and Hemophilia B, Study B784005, Cohort OP-PPX
L1570: Source: Reviewer Table
L1571: Abbreviations: N, overall number of participants; STD, standard deviation; Mn, minimum; Max, maximum
L1572: ## 17. Clinical Safety
L1573: ## 17.1. Assessment of Safety by Observation Period Treatment (Factor On-Demand, Factor Prophylaxis)
L1574: In Section 6.3, the safety analysis pooled the OP-OD and OP-PPX cohorts. This was done because both cohorts received factor replacement during OP with the only difference being frequency of administration (on-demand versus prophylaxis) and all participants, regardless of observational phase treatment, received the same ATP marstacimab regimen; therefore, the treatment received prior to enrollment and during OP is not expected to impact the safety profile of marstacimab overall.
L1575: The demographics and baseline disease characteristics are shown in Table 106. There are differences in the age distribution between the two cohorts. A higher percentage of participants were &lt;18 years of age in the OP-PPX cohort (21%) compared to the OP-OD cohort (6%); however, a higher percentage of participants in the OP-OD cohort were 18 to 44 years of age (82%) compared to the OP-PPX cohort (52%). There are differences in site enrollment between the two cohorts, most notably, a higher percentage of participants in the OP-OD cohort were enrolled in Asia (64%) compared to the OP-PPX cohort (36%). This likely reflects standard treatment practices (on-demand versus prophylaxis use of factor) in different regions. There are some differences in number of baseline number of target joints, notably, a higher proportion of participants with \ge 3 target joints in the OP-OD cohort (36%) compared to OP-PPX (16%). This may reflect effectiveness of factor prophylaxis compared to factor OD.
L1576: Table 107 provides an overview of frequency analysis of safety during the ATP with marstacimab in Study B7841005, by cohort. The overall rate of treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs) was higher in the OP-PPX (75%), compared to the OP-OD; however, this is primarily accounted for by an increase in mild events in the OP-PPX. While all SAEs occurred in the OP-PPX, as noted in Section 7.6.1.3, all but one of the SAEs reported in the primary safety population during the ATP for Study 7684015 were assessed as not related to marstacimab.
L1577: The difference in frequency for TEAEs based on FMQ (narrow) (Table 108) between cohorts was &lt;10% for all FMQs (narrow) with the exception of viral infection, which occurred in 27% of participants in the OP-PPX cohort compared to 6% in the OP-OD cohort, and hemorrhage, which occurred in 13% of participants in the OP-PPX cohort compared to 3% in the OP-OD cohort. The higher frequency of viral infections in the OP-PPX cohort may reflect a slightly older population or may be due to chance, but based on mechanism of action is unlikely to be due to marstacimab. The event of hemorrhage is related to underlying disease.
L1578: Based on a comparison of safety profiles in the OP-OD and OP-PPX cohorts, the review team concluded that there were no clinically meaningful differences and reporting in the United States Prescribing Information (USPI) by prior factor therapy (i.e., by cohort) was not needed to support the safe and effective use of marstacimab.

L1607: ## 17.2. Assessment of Safety by Hemophilia Type
L1608: In Section 6.3, the primary safety population includes participants with HA and HB. Based on the similarity of the underlying mechanism of HA and HB and the mechanism of action of marstacimab, Study B7841005 does not enroll participants HA and HB to separate cohorts. The safety profile is not expected to be different in these two subpopulations.
L1609: Table 109 provides a frequency-based overview of TEAEs by hemophilia type reported in the safety population during the active treatment phase for Study B7841005. Overall, the analysis of TEAEs by hemophilia type showed no significant differences in the frequency of adverse events between the two groups (HA versus HB). As noted above, only one SAE was assessed as related to marstacimab, and therefore, analysis of SAEs by subgroup is not meaningful. Table 110 presents the most common TEAEs by system organ class (SOC) and preferred term (PT) and hemophilia type. There are no significant differences in frequency for any specific PT or SOC between HA and HB.
L1610: Based on this subgroup analysis, the review team concludes that reporting in the USPI by hemophilia subtype not needed for the safe and effective use of marstacimab.

L1631: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1632: A clinical inspection summary for BLA 761369 was completed by the Division of Clinical Compliance Evaluation in the Office of Scientific Investigations with a final signature date of August 1, 2024. Four clinical sites were inspected for Study B7841005 (Site 1123 [Nirmal Kumar Choraria, MD (Surat, Gujarat India)], Site 1035 [Young Shil Park, MD (Seoul, South Korea), Site 1127 [Javier Morales Adrian, MD (Merida, Yucatan, Mexico) and Site 1013 [Laurent Frenzel, MD (Paris,France)]]).
L1633: The Office of Scientific Investigations concluded that, based on the inspections, the study data derived from these four clinical sites are considered reliable and are acceptable. The study data submitted to the Agency for assessment appears acceptable in support of the proposed indication.

L1723: Diagram illustrating the Intrinsic and Extrinsic Coagulation Pathways leading to Fibrin formation.
Intrinsic Pathway: Factor XII (XII) is activated by surface contact to XIIa. XIIa activates Factor XI (XI) to XIa. XIa activates Factor IX (IX) to IXa. IXa activates Factor X (X) to Xa.
Extrinsic Pathway: Factor VII (VII) is activated by vascular injury to VIIa. VIIa, in complex with Tissue Factor (TF), activates Factor X (X) to Xa.
Common Pathway: Factor Xa (Xa) activates Prothrombin to Thrombin. Thrombin converts Fibrinogen to Fibrin.
The diagram also shows the inhibition of the Extrinsic Pathway by TFPI (Tissue Factor Pathway Inhibitor).
L1724: Figure 2 is a schematic diagram illustrating the site of action of TF, TFPI, and Marstacimab in the extrinsic coagulation pathway. The diagram shows the sequential steps of coagulation factor activation and the regulatory role of TFPI and Marstacimab.
Initiation: Tissue Factor (TF) binds Factor VIIa (FVIIa) on a cell membrane.
Activation: The TF/FVIIa complex activates Factor X (FX).
Release: Activated Factor Xa (FXa) is released into the surrounding tissue.
Propagation: FXa propagates coagulation by activating Factor XI (FXI).
Coagulation: FXa promotes coagulation via the common coagulation pathway.
Regulation: Plasma Tissue Factor Pathway Inhibitor (TFPI) binds to the TF/FVIIa/FXa complex, inhibiting TF and FXa activity.
Inhibition: Marstacimab binds to TFPI via its K2 domain, inhibiting TFPI's ability to regulate the TF/FVIIa/FXa complex, thereby increasing the activity of TF and FXa.
L1725: Figure 3 displays three graphs showing the dose-dependent decrease in clotting time (Minutes) by Marstacimab (PF-06741086) using whole blood from Donor 1, Donor 2, and Donor 3. The x-axis shows concentrations of IgG1 (left side) and PF-06741086 (right side) in µg/mL. The y-axis shows clotting time in Minutes. In all three graphs, increasing concentrations of PF-06741086 lead to a decrease in clotting time, indicating a dose-dependent effect.
L1726: Figure 4 displays three bar charts showing the dose-dependent decrease in blood volume loss and duration of effect in mouse models of Hemophilia A and B. The left chart (Hemophilia A Mouse) shows Volume blood loss (uL) versus Marstacimab dose (mg/kg). The middle chart (Hemophilia A Mouse) shows Volume blood loss (microliter) versus Time (hour) for a 6 mg/kg dose. The right chart (Hemophilia B Mouse) shows Volume blood loss (microliter) versus Time (hour) for a 6 mg/kg dose. Saline is the control.

L1729: Flowchart illustrating the study schema for Noninhibitor Cohorts (Study B7841005).
The study consists of an Observational Phase (OP) of 6 Months followed by an Active Treatment Phase (ATP) of 12 Months.
Participants undergo a 42 Days Screening period.
Two cohorts are formed based on prior treatment during the OP:
Cohort OP-OD: HA or HB Without Inhibitors, 6 months Prior Factor On-Demand. This cohort receives Factor (FVIII, FIX) On-Demand treatment during the OP, followed by Marstacimab Prophylaxis during the ATP.
Cohort OP-PPX: HA or HB Without Inhibitors, 6 months Prior Factor Prophylaxis. This cohort receives Factor (FVIII, FIX) Prophylaxis during the OP, followed by Marstacimab Prophylaxis during the ATP.
Both cohorts proceed to the Study B7841007 Long term Extension Marstacimab Prophylaxis phase.

L1734: Figure 12: Schematic Pathway in the Coagulation Cascade and the Role of Marstacimab in the Extrinsic Pathway. The diagram illustrates the Intrinsic Pathway (XII, XI, IX, X) and the Extrinsic Pathway (VII, Vila + TF, X). Both pathways converge on Factor X (Xa) activation, leading to Prothrombin, Thrombin, Fibrinogen, and Fibrin. TFPI inhibits the Extrinsic Pathway. Marstacimab is shown binding to TFPI, neutralizing its inhibitory effect on the FVIIa/TF/Factor X (FXa) complex, thereby promoting clot formation.
L1735: Figure 13 displays two graphs illustrating the dose-dependent inhibition of TFPI activity by PF-06741086 (marstacimab).
The left graph, titled "PF-06741086 Neutralization of TFPI FXa Inhibition," shows % Activity versus Log (ng/mL). PF-06741086 exhibits dose-dependent inhibition, reaching approximately 100% activity at concentrations \ge 16 ng/mL. IgG1 shows minimal activity.
The right graph, titled "PF-06741086 TFPI Inhibition in a FVIIa/TF/FX Activity Assay," shows % Activity versus Log (ng/mL). PF-06741086 exhibits dose-dependent inhibition, reaching approximately 100% activity at concentrations \ge 16 ng/mL. IgG1 shows minimal activity.
L1736: Figure 14 displays two graphs showing the effect of PF-06741086 (marstacimab) on coagulation times in nonhemophilic platelet-poor human plasma.
The left graph, titled "Pooled Human Plasma dPT," plots dPT (seconds) versus concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale. The dPT decreases dose-dependently as the concentration of PF-06741086 increases, ranging from approximately 105 seconds at 0.1 ng/mL to 90 seconds at 100,000 ng/mL.
The right graph, titled "Pooled Human Plasma aPTT," plots aPTT (seconds) versus concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale. The aPTT remains relatively constant around 35 seconds across the tested concentrations (0.1 to 100,000 ng/mL) for PF-06741086, IgG1, and PBS controls.
Legend for the right graph: PF-06741086 (solid line with dots), IgG1 (dashed line with squares), PBS (dotted line with triangles).
L1737: Figure 15 displays two graphs showing the dose-dependent increase in peak thrombin concentration (nM) over time (Minutes) in nonhemophilic platelet-poor human plasma.
The left graph, titled "PF-06741086," shows that increasing concentrations of PF-06741086 (16 \mug/mL, 1.6 \mug/mL, 0.16 \mug/mL, 0.08 \mug/mL, 0.016 \mug/mL, and 0.0016 \mug/mL) lead to a dose-dependent increase in peak thrombin generation, reaching up to 220 nM at 16 \mug/mL. The "No Ab" control shows minimal thrombin generation.
The right graph, titled "IgG1 Control," shows that increasing concentrations of IgG1 (16 \mug/mL, 1.6 \mug/mL, 0.16 \mug/mL, 0.08 \mug/mL, 0.016 \mug/mL, and 0.0016 \mug/mL) do not significantly increase peak thrombin generation compared to the "No Ab" control, which peaks around 110 nM.

L1764: Figure 41 displays two plots related to the Exposure-Response (E-R) analysis of Annualized Bleeding Rate (ABR) versus average plasma concentration (Cavg). The top plot is a scatter plot showing ABR (Y-axis, 0 to 30) versus Cavg (ng/mL, X-axis, 0 to 120,000). Data points are colored by dose: 150 mg (300 mg LD, red), 300 mg (green), and 450 mg (blue). A blue line represents the Loess fit, showing a decreasing trend of ABR with increasing Cavg. The bottom plot is a box plot showing the distribution of Cavg (ng/mL) for the three doses (150 mg (300 mg LD), 300 mg, and 450 mg). The 450 mg dose shows the highest median Cavg, followed by 300 mg, and 150 mg (300 mg LD) shows the lowest median Cavg.

L1766: Phase 3 Study Schematic Diagram:
The study is divided into a 6-Month Observational Phase and a 12-Month Active Treatment Phase (using PF-06741086 prophylaxis).
6-Month Observational Phase:
On-demand Participants, All Sites
Prophylaxis Participants, All Sites
Participants enter the study via a Screening period (45 days).
Cohorts (n values are minimum planned):
Hem A Inhibitor Cohort (n \ge 35)
Hem B Inhibitor Cohort (n \ge 10)
Hem A Non-Inhibitor Cohort (n \ge 22)*
Hem B Non-Inhibitor Cohort (n \ge 6)*
Hem A Non-Inhibitor Cohort (n \ge 54)*
Hem B Non-Inhibitor Cohort (n \ge 13)*
12-Month Active Treatment Phase: PF-06741086 prophylaxis.
Total Planned Participants:
Minimum planned inhibitor participants: N \ge 45 (~35 with Hem A and ~10 Hem B)
Minimum planned non-inhibitor participants: N \ge 100 (~80 with Hem A and ~20 with Hem B)
